# pstack usage audit — 25 October 2026

## Spend

**$27.163224958 settled** across the three audited machine runs, plus **$0.097498400 held** for unknown outcomes. **$27.260723358 all-in**, 614 requests, read from each run's `ledger.json` at published API prices.

| Card | Run | Settled | Held | Requests |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| JUL-252 | `jobs/jul-252-e3f3a24ba080b73768bef649b1d162df` | $21.218313334 | $0.097498400 | 464 |
| JUL-269 | `jobs/jul-269-aee236e60da67d7efe4cda30d58b1a67` | $5.269533176 | $0 | 130 |
| JUL-268 | `jobs/jul-268-54fc4689956a25fb7b4aa774ba0c93ae` | $0.675378448 | $0 | 20 |
| **Total** | | **$27.163224958** | **$0.097498400** | **614** |

By model: Claude Opus 5.5 $26.275940800, DeepSeek V4.1 Flash $0.245258208, GPT-6 Sol $0.542350400, Claude Haiku 5.5 $0.099675550.

JUL-252's figure is the card's whole ledger, so it includes the earlier attempts on the same card. AGENTS.md:9 requires spend to be recorded and reported per card, and the card is the unit the ledger keeps. This audit ran 62 DeepSeek V4.1 Flash helpers; its own usage is not in a card ledger.

## What was audited

Every file in the 62 skill folders under `.agents/skills/`, line by line: `poteto-mode` (SKILL.md, all 24 playbooks, its reference and first-party scripts), every routed skill, every principle leaf, every reference file and every playbook. Two things are not tabled: `poteto-mode/scripts/node_modules/`, which is vendored dependency code rather than pstack instructions, and the two root metadata files `PSTACK-LICENSE.txt` and `PSTACK-SOURCE.md`, which record provenance rather than instruct an agent.

Each instruction was compared with what Julia actually does: `AGENTS.md`, `docs/plans/path-forward.md`, `docs/principles/inverse-pyramid.md`, the machine code under `ops/build-runner/` and `ops/mastra-agents/`, `~/.agents/pstack-models.md`, the other skill folders, and the checklists, decision logs and review folders of the last three machine runs on 25 October (JUL-269, JUL-268, JUL-252). Run artifacts are cited as `jobs/<run-id>/...`, meaning `~/.local/state/julia-jul231/jobs-attempt-2/<run-id>/...`.

**2,544 instructions** were classified:

| Status | Rows | Share |
| --- | --- | --- |
| used as intended | 483 | 19.0% |
| used differently | 601 | 23.6% |
| not used | 961 | 37.8% |
| doesn't apply here | 354 | 13.9% |
| replaced by ours | 145 | 5.7% |

The shape is consistent across folders: the machine injects exactly two skill files, poteto-mode (`ops/build-runner/worker.mjs:84-85`) and adversarial-review for the evidence review (`ops/build-runner/worker.mjs:26-27`). Everything else is reachable only if the model opens it by name, or not at all. The machine runs Claude Haiku 5.5 as orchestrator, DeepSeek V4.1 Flash as coder and an OpenAI model as reviewer (`ops/build-runner/machine-settings.json:11-19`), which is not the split written in `docs/plans/path-forward.md:96` (Sol for the main session and hardest work, DeepSeek for everyday coding and swarm workers, panels of one of each). No panel is required and the reviewer gets no helpers (`ops/build-runner/worker.mjs:209-216`).

## Citation conventions

- `SKILL.md`, `references/...`, `scripts/...`, `features/...`, `tests/...` inside a skill's table are relative to that skill's folder.
- A bare playbook name such as `feature.md:12` is relative to `.agents/skills/poteto-mode/playbooks/`.
- A bare machine filename such as `worker.mjs:170`, `evidence-review.mjs:150` or `pstack-session.mjs:54` is relative to `ops/build-runner/`.
- `jobs/<run-id>/...` is `jobs/<run-id>/...`.
- Every resolvable citation in the tables (5,916 of them) was machine-checked to point at an existing file and an in-range line. The 589 bare filenames left are shorthand inside a longer row and resolve by the rules above.

## Tables

Each table covers every instruction in that folder. `Instruction` is a short paraphrase, not a quote; the skill-side citation is the line the paraphrase comes from.

## poteto-mode

Reached in Julia: every build — one pstack session reads the full skill text (`ops/build-runner/pstack-session.mjs:56`) which the host also injects as authoritative (`ops/build-runner/worker.mjs:84-85`), while the playbook it must follow is fixed from the card label (`ops/build-runner/evidence-review.mjs:4-26`) and judged at `.builder-home/proof/checklist.md` (`ops/build-runner/evidence-review.mjs:142-166`). Run artifacts cited below are the three 25 Oct jobs under `jobs/` (JUL-269, JUL-268, JUL-252); `scripts/node_modules/` is vendored and not audited.

## adversarial-review

Reached in Julia: this is the evidence review's own instruction file, named `REVIEW_SKILL` (`ops/build-runner/worker.mjs:26-27`), passed as the reviewer session's host instructions (`ops/build-runner/worker.mjs:209`) and run as a second pstack-style session with no helpers (`ops/build-runner/worker.mjs:204-216`) after every build (`ops/build-runner/worker.mjs:397-401`), judging each criterion and playbook adherence through `.builder-home/review/verdicts.json` (`ops/build-runner/evidence-review.mjs:142-166`). All three review rounds in the 25 Oct runs read it and wrote that verdicts file in their review trees; the run citations below are relative to the job root `jobs/`.

| # | Instruction (short) | Status | Julia evidence | Skill evidence | Note |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | Frontmatter `name: adversarial-review`: the skill is addressed by this name | used as intended | `ops/build-runner/worker.mjs:26-27` | `SKILL.md:2` | The host hard-wires the path rather than looking it up, but the folder name matches and both real reviews also loaded it by that name. |
| 2 | Description: independently challenge a checked candidate before an evidence-backed verdict | used differently | `ops/build-runner/worker.mjs:209`; `ops/build-runner/worker.mjs:397-401` | `SKILL.md:3` | No agent chooses it: the host injects the text as the reviewer's host instructions and starts the stage after every build. |
| 3 | Review only the supplied specification, pinned code and underlying evidence | used as intended | `ops/build-runner/worker.mjs:193-203` | `SKILL.md:8-9` | The session's whole working tree is a `git archive` of the candidate commit plus a written `.builder-home/proof` folder. |
| 4 | Do not read builder conversations | used differently | `ops/build-runner/worker.mjs:173`; `ops/build-runner/worker.mjs:175` | `SKILL.md:9` | No transcript is supplied, but the builder's checklist and decision log are mandatory proof the reviewer must judge (`evidence-review.mjs:150`). |
| 5 | Do not repair the candidate | used differently | `ops/build-runner/worker.mjs:213-215` | `SKILL.md:9-10` | Nothing checks the tree; the candidate commit is fixed before review (`:370`) and the host reads only `verdicts.json`, so a repair cannot change the verdict. |
| 6 | Do not mutate Git or trackers, or publish, merge, deploy | used as intended | `ops/build-runner/worker.mjs:199-200`; `ops/build-runner/adapter.mjs:172` | `SKILL.md:9-10` | The export carries no `.git`, and MCP, plugins, hooks and GitHub signals are off; publishing is marked out of scope (`pstack-session.mjs:54`). |
| 7 | Use the runner's output contract | used as intended | `ops/build-runner/evidence-review.mjs:160-161` | `SKILL.md:10` | One JSON shape is named in the prompt and parsed field by field; unknown criteria, duplicates and unrecognised verdicts are rejected (`:92-121`). |
| 8 | Do not assume a particular vendor, CLI, tool or model | used as intended | `ops/build-runner/worker.mjs:223-227`; `ops/build-runner/machine-settings.json:12-18` | `SKILL.md:10-11` | The reviewer is any configured model from another company than the orchestrator and coder; both real reviews ran on `openai/gpt-6-sol` against a DeepSeek builder. |

## automate-me

Reached in Julia: not reached by any card. Every build session is told to read poteto-mode and select its playbook (`ops/build-runner/pstack-session.mjs:56`) with that text injected as authoritative (`ops/build-runner/worker.mjs:84-85`). automate-me appears only as a chat-level route (`poteto-help/SKILL.md:95`, `recall/SKILL.md:23`) and once as a bare folder name in a run's `.agents/skills` listing (`docs/agents/jul-263/proof/attempt-8-jul267-compressed/final-job.json:5295`).

Reached in Julia: `how` is Feature step 1 (`.agents/skills/poteto-mode/playbooks/feature.md:5`) and the Investigation stage's route (`.agents/skills/poteto-mode/playbooks/investigation.md:7`), so a build can reach it and the second failed review does. ## in-your-own-words

Reached in Julia: The file is mounted with the rest of the catalog in every session (`ops/build-runner/adapter.mjs:167`, `:175`), but nothing names or routes to it: no AGENTS.md line, no path-forward step, no poteto-mode trigger or playbook, and no session prompt. The one thing it asks for is already fixed before the build as the card's Outcome (`docs/plans/path-forward.md:52`).

| # | Instruction (short) | Status | Julia evidence | Skill evidence | Note |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | Frontmatter `name: in-your-own-words`: installed and addressed under this name | used as intended | `scripts/check-skill-names.mjs:21-25` | `SKILL.md:2` | The check parses every `name:` under `.agents/skills` and `.claude/skills` and CI fails on duplicates (`AGENTS.md:30`). No Julia code addresses this skill by name. |
| 2 | Description states its purpose: check the agent understood the goal and the problem | used as intended | `.agents/skills/poteto-help/SKILL.md:69` | `SKILL.md:3` | The repo's command index carries that one-line purpose for it. No session picks a skill by description, and poteto-mode's trigger list (`.agents/skills/poteto-mode/SKILL.md:17-36`) omits it. |
| 3 | Invoked as the `/in-your-own-words` command | not used | `ops/build-runner/pstack-session.mjs:56` | `SKILL.md:3` | Mounted but never named. A card session has no human turn that could type it, and all three 25 Oct checklists open at `how`, not at a restatement. |
| 4 | `disable-model-invocation: true`: the model never starts it on its own | not used | `ops/build-runner/adapter.mjs:167` | `SKILL.md:4` | The session mounts the whole catalog and lists it (`:175`); no code in `ops/` or `scripts/` reads the flag, so the guard is prose only. Nothing self-started it either. |
| 5 | Restate in your own words the user's goals and the problem they are trying to solve | used differently | `docs/plans/path-forward.md:52` | `SKILL.md:7` | The statement happens upstream: Outcome written at Idea and settled by Todd (`:25-26`). The build gets card JSON and never restates; misreads surface at evidence review (`:58`). |

## ketchup

Reached in Julia: only as a catalog entry and as an operator-facing reply in playbooks a card never runs. The repo's command index names `/ketchup` and links it (`.agents/skills/poteto-help/SKILL.md:75`), and the autopilot playbooks tell a coordinator to catch the operator up per ketchup (`.agents/skills/poteto-mode/playbooks/autopilot-stack.md:12`). Every 25 Oct run was a Feature card whose only human-facing text is the host's Linear verdict note, generated at `ops/build-runner/evidence-review.mjs:127-139` and posted at `ops/build-runner/worker.mjs:235`.

| # | Instruction (short) | Status | Julia evidence | Skill evidence | Note |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | Frontmatter `name: ketchup`: the skill is addressed by this name | used as intended | `.agents/skills/poteto-help/SKILL.md:75`; `scripts/check-skill-names.mjs:21-25` | `SKILL.md:2` | The index links `/ketchup` to this file and the name check keeps it unique across `.agents/skills` (`AGENTS.md:30`). No machine code calls it. |
| 2 | Frontmatter description: catch this chat up plainly, every waiting item decidable from the reply alone | used as intended | `.agents/skills/poteto-help/SKILL.md:75`; `.agents/skills/poteto-help/SKILL.md:109` | `SKILL.md:3` | The index row carries the same purpose and keeps it distinct from `/recall`. No card session selects a skill by description. |
| 3 | `disable-model-invocation: true`: user-invoked only, never model-invoked | doesn't apply here | `ops/build-runner/adapter.mjs:167` | `SKILL.md:4`; `.agents/skills/poteto-mode/SKILL.md:103-113` | The harness mapping has no invocation switch. Julia mounts the whole `.agents/skills` tree as the session catalog (`adapter.mjs:167`, `:175`), so every skill is registered alike. |
| 4 | Catch the user up on what changed in this chat since their last message before `/ketchup` | doesn't apply here | `ops/build-runner/pstack-session.mjs:56` | `SKILL.md:9`; `.agents/skills/poteto-mode/SKILL.md:103-113` | No machine code names it. Only the autopilot playbooks invoke ketchup, and a run's playbook comes from its card label (`evidence-review.mjs:14-20`), Feature in all three runs. |
| 5 | Help them understand each change, not only where it stands | not used | `ops/build-runner/evidence-review.mjs:134-137` | `SKILL.md:9` | Absent. The only human-facing text is a verdict per criterion with its proof path. Nothing explains what a change means to the reader. |
| 6 | Lay out every call waiting on them so they can decide it from the reply alone | replaced by ours | `ops/build-runner/evidence-review.mjs:127-139` | `SKILL.md:9` | The host writes and posts that note itself (`worker.mjs:234-235`): each criterion, its verdict and proof file, then `Next: <stage>`. ## maintain-formal-verification-skill

Reached in Julia: the folder is installed verbatim from pstack and its hash is pinned (`.agents/skills/PSTACK-SOURCE.md:3`, `scripts/agent-docs.test.mjs:95`), and `.agents/skills` is registered as the session's skills root (`ops/build-runner/adapter.mjs:167`). A build is told only to read poteto-mode and its own playbook (`ops/build-runner/pstack-session.mjs:56`), with that text injected as authoritative (`ops/build-runner/worker.mjs:84-85`), so no run opened it; the only contact in a run artifact is a directory listing of `.agents/skills` (`docs/agents/jul-263/proof/attempt-8-jul267-compressed/final-job.json:5295`).

| # | Instruction (short) | Status | Julia evidence | Skill evidence | Note |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | A stale check can keep passing on code that no longer exists, and that pass looks like a proof | used differently | `ops/build-runner/evidence-review.mjs:154` | `SKILL.md:9` | A criterion is proven only by a named proof file showing it holds in this candidate tree, so a claim carried over from older code fails the review. |
| 2 | This skill is the upkeep loop for a verify skill that `/create-formal-verification-skill` generated | replaced by ours | `.agents/skills/verify-julia/SKILL.md:96-98` | `SKILL.md:9` | `verify-julia` names its own upkeep route (`/maintain-verification-skill`); formal verification was retired to pstack at build (`docs/plans/path-forward.md:35`), so no formal skill exists. |
| 3 | It handles the changes that skipped the verify skill: merges, edits without it, drift over many commits | used differently | `.agents/skills/poteto-mode/playbooks/shipping.md:7` | `SKILL.md:9` | Drift after a rebase or merge is caught by re-checking a verdict against the current head with `git patch-id`, not against a sync commit. |

## poteto-help

Reached in Julia: only as an unused catalog entry. The whole `.agents/skills` tree is mounted as the session's skill list (`ops/build-runner/adapter.mjs:167`, listed at `:175`), but every build is told to read poteto-mode and nothing else (`ops/build-runner/pstack-session.mjs:56`) and every evidence review is given adversarial-review (`ops/build-runner/worker.mjs:26-27`, `:209-216`); the readback recorded no reader and no configured unit for this file (`docs/agents/jul-253/ovh-readback/readback.jsonl:17300`), and none of the three 25 Oct runs opened it.

## recall

Reached in Julia: only in the plan, which gives `/recall` to the Idea step so the planner agent catches up on failed and reverted attempts (`docs/plans/path-forward.md:25`, `:90`). No machine code or run opens it: the build session is told to read poteto-mode only (`ops/build-runner/pstack-session.mjs:56`), and the only `recall` mentions in the three 25 Oct runs are builds quoting that plan line (`jobs/jul-269-aee236e60da67d7efe4cda30d58b1a67/events.jsonl:271`, `jul-252-e3f3a24ba080b73768bef649b1d162df/events.jsonl:8`).

| # | Instruction (short) | Status | Julia evidence | Skill evidence | Note |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | Use for "recall my work on X", "catch me up", "where did I leave off", before starting work | not used | `docs/plans/path-forward.md:25` | `SKILL.md:3` | The plan assigns it to the Idea step for the planner agent. No machine code, checklist line or run artifact invokes it. |
| 2 | Frontmatter `disable-model-invocation: true`: only a human typing the command starts it | not used | `ops/build-runner/pstack-session.mjs:56` | `SKILL.md:4` | No human sits in a build session and no build is offered recall, so nothing can start it. No repo script reads this flag. |
| 3 | Before starting or resuming work, rebuild recent context and hand back a tight capsule | not used | `ops/build-runner/pstack-session.mjs:52-56` | `SKILL.md:9` | A build starts from the saved card, the injected poteto-mode text and the worktree at a base commit. History is never rebuilt. |
| 4 | Keep it tight: read only what the in-scope threads need, then stop | not used | `ops/build-runner/worker.mjs:337` | `SKILL.md:11` | No recall read to bound. The only bound on reading is the 100-turn cap. |
| 5 | Context lives in chat history plus the shared record the why skill searches, not transcripts alone | not used | `ops/build-runner/pstack-session.mjs:56` | `SKILL.md:13` | No sweep of source control, issue tracker, chat, docs or error tracking. A build sees the card and the repo only. |
| 6 | Transcripts are JSONL: find the active workspace's directory for your harness | doesn't apply here | `ops/build-runner/worker.mjs:92-93` | `.agents/skills/poteto-mode/SKILL.md:103-113` | This harness keeps a session in SQLite at `state/sdk/mastra.db` in the job dir, not JSONL transcripts. ## setup-pstack

Reached in Julia: as a maintained file rather than a skill run. `ops/build-runner/pstack-models.md:1-21` is the exact shape this skill writes, read at `ops/build-runner/worker.mjs:81` and seeded as `.agents/pstack-models.md` into the session home at `ops/build-runner/worker.mjs:104-112`. The chat's own copy is `~/.agents/pstack-models.md:1-20` (every line `inherit-parent`), and `docs/plans/path-forward.md:107` makes these role lines the intended replacement for the orchestrator/coder pair.

## tdd

Reached in Julia: `CODING_STANDARDS.md:17` names it as the build's test-first rule and the repo index gives it one line (`poteto-help/SKILL.md:81`), but the only route into a card run is the Bug fix playbook's step 5 (`poteto-mode/playbooks/bug-fix.md:11`), which the remediation prompt names (`ops/build-runner/evidence-review.mjs:169-171`). The host never reads this file: `ops/build-runner/worker.mjs:84-85` injects only poteto-mode, so the cadence arrives as the playbook's restatement (`docs/agents/jul-252/proof/review-playbook-adherence-2026-10-25/review-1/checklist.md:15-16`) and no run cites the SKILL.md itself.

| # | Instruction (short) | Status | Julia evidence | Skill evidence | Note |

| 2 | Description: use only when TDD is asked or a cheap local test exists; skip when expensive or unclear | used differently | `CODING_STANDARDS.md:17` | `SKILL.md:3` | No selector: the session chooses from the mounted catalog (`adapter.mjs:167`). The gate is restated at `bug-fix.md:11`; the standards make test-first unconditional for builds. |
| 3 | `disable-model-invocation: true`: user-invoked only, never model-invoked | doesn't apply here | `ops/build-runner/adapter.mjs:167` | `SKILL.md:4`; `.agents/skills/poteto-mode/SKILL.md:103-113` | The mapping has no invocation switch and a card gets no slash command. Julia registers the whole `.agents/skills` tree as the session catalog (`adapter.mjs:167`, `:175`); no repo code reads the flag. |
| 4 | Make the broken behavior executable before production code: a test that fails, then passes | used as intended | `.agents/skills/poteto-mode/playbooks/bug-fix.md:11` | `SKILL.md:9` | Playbook step 5 orders the commits. The JUL-270 checklist records the spec commit 97929a3 before the fix commit a64b205 (`review-1/checklist.md:16`). |
| 5 | Do not force a test when impractical; skip it and use the closest useful verification instead | used as intended | `.agents/skills/poteto-mode/playbooks/bug-fix.md:11`; `.agents/skills/verify-julia/SKILL.md:8` | `SKILL.md:11` | Same skip clause in the playbook. The JUL-270 run fell back to served HTML when Chromium could not start and flagged the unrun spec (`review-1/checklist.md:14`). |

## technical-writing

## typescript-best-practices

Reached in Julia only by pointer, never opened by a card: the help index lists it as a user slash command (`.agents/skills/poteto-help/SKILL.md:82`) and the type-system principle sends TypeScript syntax here (`.agents/skills/principle-type-system-discipline/SKILL.md:9`), while the build prompt names only poteto-mode (`ops/build-runner/pstack-session.mjs:56`) and no checklist under `docs/agents/` names it. Its `paths` and `disable-model-invocation` frontmatter is a Cursor mechanism the harness mapping does not reproduce (`.agents/skills/poteto-mode/SKILL.md:103-113`), and app code is `.jsx`/`.js` (`app/page.jsx:1`), TypeScript living in a legacy package (`ops/mastra-agents/reviewer/README.md:3-7`).

| # | Instruction (short) | Status | Julia evidence | Skill evidence | Note |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | Use the skill when reading or editing any `.ts` or `.tsx` file | used differently | `.agents/skills/poteto-help/SKILL.md:82` | `SKILL.md:3` | Listed as a user slash command in the help index. No host instruction or playbook step opens it for a `.ts` edit, and no card checklist under `docs/agents/` names it. |
| 2 | `paths: ["**/*.ts", "**/*.tsx"]` attaches the skill by file glob | doesn't apply here | `ops/build-runner/pstack-session.mjs:52-56`; `ops/build-runner/worker.mjs:84-85` | `SKILL.md:4`; `.agents/skills/poteto-mode/SKILL.md:103-113` | The mapping has no path-scoped attachment. Julia injects the poteto-mode text as authoritative and nothing loads a skill by file extension. |
| 3 | `disable-model-invocation: true` means only an explicit path opens the skill | doesn't apply here | `ops/build-runner/pstack-session.mjs:56`; `scripts/check-skill-names.mjs:23-24` | `SKILL.md:5`; `.agents/skills/poteto-mode/SKILL.md:103-113` | The harness mapping has no invocation switch, and the only frontmatter reader takes `name` alone, so this flag and `paths` are inert here. |
| 4 | Apply the **type-system-discipline** principle skill first | used differently | `.agents/skills/principle-type-system-discipline/SKILL.md:9`; `.agents/skills/poteto-mode/SKILL.md:59` | `SKILL.md:10`; `references/patterns.md:3` | The principle skill is installed, routed by poteto-mode and points back here. The build prompt names only poteto-mode, so no session read either file. |
| 5 | Model variants with a `kind` literal discriminant; no optional-field bags | used differently | `ops/mastra-agents/reviewer/src/mastra/contracts.ts:120`; `contracts.ts:231` | `SKILL.md:14`; `references/patterns.md:28-41` | Variants are zod discriminated unions, but on three different keys, and partial wire shapes stay optional-field bags (`contracts.ts:14-17`, `agent-codex.ts:7`). |
| 6 | Pick one discriminant name (`kind`, `type`, `tag`) and stick to it | not used | `ops/mastra-agents/reviewer/src/mastra/contracts.ts:120-231` | `references/patterns.md:43` | One file uses `admitted`, `action` and `status` as discriminants, and events key on `kind` (`agent-events.ts:11`). No script or run checks the name. |
| 7 | Brand primitives with `& { readonly __brand: "X" }`, validated once at the boundary | used differently | `.agents/skills/poteto-mode/scripts/watch-pr/types.ts:1-2`; `types.ts:7-10` | `SKILL.md:15`; `references/patterns.md:5-24` | The one brand in the repo keys on a `unique symbol`, not the `__brand` string, and is applied by `as` after a check (`types.ts:10`). Reviewer package has no brand. |
| 8 | Match the `readonly __brand: 'X'` shape; don't invent a new convention | used differently | `.agents/skills/poteto-mode/scripts/watch-pr/types.ts:1-2` | `references/patterns.md:26` | The repo's single brand invents a symbol key instead of this shape, so the convention callers are told to match does not exist here. |
| 9 | Constructive modeling: `[T, ...T[]]` non-empty, `[T, T][]` even, start plus duration | used differently | `.agents/skills/poteto-mode/scripts/watch-pr/types.ts:3-6` | `SKILL.md:16`; `references/patterns.md:45-64` | Only the non-empty tuple exists, as `NonEmpty<T>` plus `nonEmpty()`. No even-length or start-plus-duration type appears in any TypeScript. |
| 10 | Where a plain `T[]` arrives, narrow once with a guard so the fact travels in the type | used as intended | `.agents/skills/poteto-mode/scripts/watch-pr/types.ts:4-6`; `cli.ts:59` | `references/patterns.md:66-70` | `nonEmpty()` is that guard: callers get a `NonEmpty<T>` or null and the strengthened type travels. It comes from pstack's own tooling, not from a card. |
| 11 | Even length as pairs: `[T, T][]` | not used | `.agents/skills/poteto-mode/scripts/watch-pr/types.ts:3` | `references/patterns.md:75` | No pairs type exists in any TypeScript here. The nearest shape is the non-empty tuple at `types.ts:3`, which models a different invariant. |
| 12 | A time range as `start` plus `duration`, so a negative range cannot be written | not used | `ops/mastra-agents/reviewer/src/mastra/workflows/delivery.ts:51` | `references/patterns.md:80-86` | Absent. Timing is `startedAt` plus `durationMs` on trace objects, not a range type, and nothing forbids a negative duration. |
| 13 | Keep `durationMs` a plain number; brand it only if a raw number could stand for a duration | used as intended | `ops/mastra-agents/reviewer/src/mastra/workflows/delivery.ts:49-51` | `references/patterns.md:88` | Durations stay plain `number` fields on trace and failure events and nothing brands them, which is this file's own default. |
| 14 | Keep `T[]` while every operation is total; strengthen only where `!`, a cast or a throw is forced | used as intended | `.agents/skills/poteto-mode/scripts/watch-pr/types.ts:3-6`; `github.ts:672` | `SKILL.md:17`; `references/patterns.md:90-98` | `orderStack` returns `NonEmpty<PrContext>` where a first element must exist, and arrays whose operations stay total are left loose. |
| 15 | Weakening the result to a union with `undefined` is the other total signature | used as intended | `.agents/skills/poteto-mode/scripts/watch-pr/types.ts:4`; `ops/mastra-agents/reviewer/src/mastra/control-server.ts:69` | `references/patterns.md:112` | `nonEmpty()` returns a `NonEmpty<T>` or null rather than throwing, and an optional monitor is typed `Awaited<ReturnType<...>>` or undefined. |
| 16 | External data is `unknown`; narrow before use | used as intended | `ops/mastra-agents/reviewer/src/mastra/routes.ts:110`; `.agents/skills/poteto-mode/scripts/watch-pr/types.ts:7` | `SKILL.md:18`; `references/patterns.md:114-130` | No `: any`, `<any>` or `as any` appears in any authored TypeScript; boundaries take `unknown` and parse or narrow it. |
| 17 | External sources include RPC payloads, `JSON.parse`, IPC, files, env vars, database rows | used as intended | `ops/mastra-agents/reviewer/src/mastra/agent-monitor.ts:22`; `agent-codex-connection.ts:16`; `agent-activity.ts:65` | `references/patterns.md:132` | Config files, RPC stdout and database rows are each parsed out of `unknown` with a schema at the edge, which is this list applied. |
| 18 | Schemas before hand-written guards: use the repo schema library and infer the type from it | used as intended | `ops/mastra-agents/reviewer/src/mastra/agent-events.ts:18-19`; `contracts.ts:1` | `SKILL.md:19`; `references/patterns.md:134-150` | Domain types are `z.infer` of zod schemas with one parse function each. | 19 | Use `safeParse` when failure is an expected branch | used as intended | `ops/mastra-agents/reviewer/src/mastra/linear.ts:80`; `control-page.ts:180`; `card-admission-check.ts:21` | `references/patterns.md:153` | Admission and control-page reads branch on `safeParse().success`; `.parse` is kept where a bad shape is a real failure. |
| 20 | Do not add a new schema dependency for one guard; use the library the codebase trusts | used as intended | `ops/mastra-agents/reviewer/src/mastra/contracts.ts:1`; `ops/mastra-agents/reviewer/package.json:33` | `references/patterns.md:153` | One schema library, `zod/v4`, covers every schema in the package, and no second validator is installed for a single guard. |
| 21 | No `as` casts; cast only after validation | not used | `ops/mastra-agents/reviewer/src/mastra/routes.ts:124`; `routes.ts:167`; `control-server.ts:88` | `SKILL.md:20`; `references/patterns.md:155-177` | Shipped code carries `as unknown as` twice, `as never` once, and `structuredClone(roles) as Record<...>` at `verify-machine.ts:211-222`, none after validation. |
| 22 | When the type comes first, annotate the validator with the type it proves | used differently | `ops/mastra-agents/reviewer/src/mastra/agent-events.ts:18-19`; `contracts.ts:120-122` | `references/patterns.md:179-185` | The package goes the other way every time: the schema is unannotated and the type is inferred from it, so no assignment can be made to fail. |
| 23 | Refactoring an `as` out: find why inference fails, missing discriminant or wide source type | not used | `ops/mastra-agents/reviewer/src/mastra/routes.ts:124`; `routes.ts:132` | `references/patterns.md:187-192` | The casts are still there and no artifact records a cause or a refactor. `routes.ts:132` casts before any check. |
| 24 | Narrowing hierarchy: discriminant switch, then `in`, then `typeof`/`instanceof`, guard, `as` | used differently | `ops/mastra-agents/reviewer/src/mastra/routes.ts:126`; `routes.ts:132` | `SKILL.md:21`; `references/patterns.md:194-202` | A `typeof` guard and an `item is` predicate sit beside bare casts in the same function, so the order is not applied. |
| 25 | Type guards must verify the claim, and are named `isX` or `hasX` | used as intended | `ops/mastra-agents/reviewer/src/mastra/routes.ts:119-120` | `SKILL.md:22`; `references/patterns.md:211-219` | `isAgentNotInstalled(error: unknown)` checks the ENOENT code before returning true and carries the `is` prefix. |
| 26 | Prefer discriminant narrowing when possible | used as intended | `ops/mastra-agents/reviewer/src/mastra/agent-mastra.ts:13-15`; `agent-codex.ts:14` | `references/patterns.md:221` | Event mapping narrows on the payload's own `type`/`status` literal and returns per variant instead of casting. |
| 27 | Inline `const _exhaustive: never = x;` in default arms so a new variant fails the build | not used | `ops/mastra-agents/reviewer/src/mastra/contracts.ts:231` | `SKILL.md:23`; `references/patterns.md:225-257` | No `never` assignment exists in any TypeScript. | 28 | Return-style in value-returning switches, void-style in statement switches | not used | `ops/mastra-agents/reviewer/src/mastra/agent-mastra.ts:13-25` | `references/patterns.md:259` | There is no exhaustiveness switch anywhere, so neither style exists. | 29 | `satisfies` over `as`: validates without widening literal types | used as intended | `.agents/skills/poteto-mode/scripts/watch-pr/github.ts:142`; `policy.test.ts:43` | `SKILL.md:24`; `references/patterns.md:261-271` | `as const satisfies readonly T.MergeStateStatus[]` and `} satisfies PollingOptions` both appear. | 30 | Boundary validation: parse where data crosses in into a named domain type, trust inside | used as intended | `ops/mastra-agents/reviewer/src/mastra/linear.ts:80`; `routes.ts:59` | `SKILL.md:25`; `references/patterns.md:274-280` | Linear cards, RPC params and files are parsed into named schemas at the edge and the parsed type travels. `routes.ts:124` is the one trust bypass. |

| 32 | Persisted JSON: a versioned blob with a try/catch around the parse | used differently | `ops/mastra-agents/reviewer/src/mastra/connections.ts:23-24`; `verify-machine.ts:17` | `references/patterns.md:279` | Persisted records are schema-parsed inside a try/catch with no version field. ## unslop

Reached in Julia once: JUL-269's Investigation round loaded this skill with `skill_read` (`jobs/jul-269-aee236e60da67d7efe4cda30d58b1a67/events.jsonl:308`) because the required Investigation playbook names it (`.agents/skills/poteto-mode/playbooks/investigation.md:10`), then claimed in its checklist copy that it had applied the skill to `.builder-home/investigation/root-cause.md` and to its reply (`jul-269-aee236e60da67d7efe4cda30d58b1a67/events.jsonl:311`). No build, remediation or evidence-review round in the three 25 Oct jobs read it, and the Feature playbook all three builds followed has no unslop step (`.agents/skills/poteto-mode/playbooks/feature.md:1-21`).

| # | Instruction (short) | Status | Julia evidence | Skill evidence | Note |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | `disable-model-invocation: true` means only a person opens the skill | used differently | `jul-269-aee236e60da67d7efe4cda30d58b1a67/events.jsonl:308`; `ops/build-runner/worker.mjs:84-85` | `SKILL.md:4` | Nothing in `scripts/` or `ops/` reads the flag. The harness has `skill_read`, and JUL-269's session used it to load this skill by name, the model-invoked route the frontmatter disallows. |
| 2 | Description: cut AI tells from any writing | used as intended | `.agents/skills/poteto-mode/SKILL.md:26` | `SKILL.md:3` | poteto-mode routes every prose surface here and the help index lists it (`.agents/skills/poteto-help/SKILL.md:84`). The route held in the Investigation round only; no build opened it. |
| 3 | Scan for the patterns below, then rewrite | used differently | `.agents/skills/poteto-mode/SKILL.md:117` | `SKILL.md:9` | Julia forbids the cleanup pass and says write the reply clean as you draft. JUL-269 read the skill after `root-cause.md` existed, so the edit was a check, not the rewrite. |
| 4 | Preserve meaning and match the intended tone | used differently | `.agents/skills/poteto-mode/SKILL.md:122` | `SKILL.md:9` | The reply rule keeps every named section when cutting length, which covers the meaning half. No Julia rule mentions tone. |
| 5 | Rule numbers are stable ids other skills cite; a removed rule leaves a gap | used as intended | `.agents/skills/poteto-mode/SKILL.md:121` | `SKILL.md:13` | poteto-mode cites "unslop rule 14" and `SKILL.md:32` is that colon rule, so the id resolves. `.agents/skills/technical-writing/SKILL.md:19` cites rule 26 the same way. |

## verify-julia

Reached in Julia: only through CI and the reviewer rule — `.github/workflows/ci.yml:71-72` runs this skill's two specs as skill-owned browser tests, and `CODING_STANDARDS.md:6-13` tells a reviewer to inspect verify-julia launch/Doctor artifacts for a user-facing change. No build session invokes the helper itself: `ops/build-runner/pstack-session.mjs:56` names poteto-mode only, and `docs/plans/path-forward.md:43` (with `:111`) still records the rewrite for Linux and a given address as plan and deferred work.

No other browser surface exists in the repo. |

## why

Reached in Julia: `why` is named by two playbook steps only, Bug fix step 2 (`.agents/skills/poteto-mode/playbooks/bug-fix.md:8`, run as Remediation after one failed review, `ops/build-runner/evidence-review.mjs:56`) and Investigation step 1 (`.agents/skills/poteto-mode/playbooks/investigation.md:7`, run after the second failed review and immediately before the card stops, `ops/build-runner/worker.mjs:269-288`). No run has opened it: JUL-269 ticked the Bug fix step without spawning anything (`jul-269-aee236e60da67d7efe4cda30d58b1a67/events.jsonl:198`) and its Investigation wrote `skip why` (`jul-269-aee236e60da67d7efe4cda30d58b1a67/worktree/.builder-home/pstack/checklist.md:7`), so no job folder contains a single read of the folder.

## principle-attack-the-premise

Reached in Julia: Through the principle list at `.agents/skills/poteto-mode/SKILL.md:47`; JUL-269's Investigation round read the leaf (`jobs-attempt-2/jul-269-aee236e60da67d7efe4cda30d58b1a67/events.jsonl:259`), wrote down the premise both rounds shared and tested it. ## principle-prove-it-works

| 6 | Cause: an assertion looser than the contract | used as intended | `.agents/skills/adversarial-review/SKILL.md:50` | `SKILL.md:13` | The test-discrimination audit hunts weak assertions as a false positive, the same defect the first imagined edit is meant to expose. |
| 7 | Cause: the expected value computed by the code under test | used as intended | `.agents/skills/adversarial-review/SKILL.md:50` | `SKILL.md:13` | The same audit names tautological expectations, its name for an expectation the code itself produced. |
| 8 | Cause: a restated constant or prompt string that never runs the code that reads it | used as intended | `docs/agents/jul-251/follow-up-ci-design.md:21` | `SKILL.md:13` | A real design applies the principle by replacing text assertions about Windows YAML with executable fixture tests. |
| 9 | Cause: an assertion on data the test built itself | used as intended | `.agents/skills/adversarial-review/SKILL.md:54` | `SKILL.md:13` | Fixtures prove the tested machinery, not real provider behavior, and `scripts/check-no-fakes.mjs:2-7` refuses a stand-in for an outside service. |
| 10 | Change the internals: restructure under the same observables; a test that then fails is pinned | not used | `.agents/skills/poteto-mode/playbooks/refactoring.md:12` | `SKILL.md:14` | No required playbook runs this edit. Refactoring asks for equivalence, yet the JUL-269 run cited a test asserting the exact children of `<main>` as proof (`pstack-checklist.md:5`). |
| 11 | Keep a test that catches the first edit and survives the second; rewrite or delete the rest | used differently | `CODING_STANDARDS.md:29` | `SKILL.md:16` | The standards keep the delete-the-rest half: every test must fail and catch something unique. Nothing asks a test to survive an internals change. |
| 12 | Drive the code through its users' interface with a concrete input | used as intended | `docs/agents/jul-231/report.md:24`; `CODING_STANDARDS.md:21` | `SKILL.md:16` | A real report records the principle keeping the CI checks at the CLI boundary, and the standards ask for coverage through the appropriate interfaces. |
| 13 | Derive the expected value from the requirement, not from a run of the code | used as intended | `ops/build-runner/evidence-review.mjs:154` | `SKILL.md:16` | The reviewer starts every criterion unproven and calls testing something else "wrong thing tested", so the criterion supplies the expectation. |
| 14 | Assert what the contract promises, no less and no more | used differently | `CODING_STANDARDS.md:21` | `SKILL.md:16` | The no-less half is checked, since assertions must detect the relevant failure. No-more has no counterpart, so an over-asserting test passes review. |
| 15 | The contract decides, not the shape of the assertion | not used | `.agents/skills/adversarial-review/SKILL.md:49-51` | `SKILL.md:18` | Absent. The audit asks whether a test would detect wrong behavior, never whether the chosen assertion shape matches what the contract promised. |
| 16 | An absence assertion is behavior when the contract promises it; pair it with a positive case | replaced by ours | `scripts/check-no-fakes.mjs:2-4`; `CODING_STANDARDS.md:22` | `SKILL.md:18` | Julia refuses a stand-in for an outside service and demands the real service, so the client-got-no-call test is replaced rather than paired with a positive. |
| 17 | The payload a mocked external service receives is behavior | replaced by ours | `scripts/check-no-fakes.mjs:2-7` | `SKILL.md:18` | A mocked external service may not exist in a tracked test, so this permission never applies here; the seam is proven against the real service (`CODING_STANDARDS.md:22`). |
| 18 | A call to an internal helper is implementation, because no user can see it | not used | `.agents/skills/adversarial-review/SKILL.md:50-51` | `SKILL.md:18` | Absent. The reviewer refuses mocks of the code under test, which is a different claim from a call assertion on an internal helper. |
| 19 | An empty list is the exact answer when no record matches, a hollow check otherwise | not used | `.agents/skills/adversarial-review/SKILL.md:50` | `SKILL.md:18` | Absent. Weak assertions is the nearest rule and it does not separate an exact empty result from a check that accepts any result. |
| 20 | An exact error message pins wording no caller reads; the error type callers branch on is the contract | not used | `CODING_STANDARDS.md:21` | `SKILL.md:18` | Absent. The standards judge error coverage only inside the changed journey's failure paths, with no rule on message text against error type. |
| 21 | A test may pin an invariant instead of a call, such as a key present in two tables | not used | `package.json:6-11` | `SKILL.md:20` | Absent. The repo has no type check and no rule asks a test to pin an invariant; the closest pins are policy scripts such as `scripts/check-no-fakes.mjs`. |

## principle-type-system-discipline

Reached in Julia: `.agents/skills/poteto-mode/SKILL.md:59` lists Type System Discipline in the principle list every build session is told to read and that is injected as authoritative (`ops/build-runner/pstack-session.mjs:56`, `ops/build-runner/worker.mjs:84-85`), and one real session loaded this leaf on demand for a `.ts` file (`docs/agents/jul-253/tailscale-proof/final/events.jsonl:116-119`). One card design names it as a chosen principle (`docs/agents/jul-251/design-a.md:43`), but no playbook step, reviewer axis or check requires it.

| # | Instruction (short) | Status | Julia evidence | Skill evidence | Note |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | Apply when designing types, reviewing a signature, or coding in any statically-typed language | used as intended | `.agents/skills/poteto-mode/SKILL.md:59` | `SKILL.md:3` | Same trigger wording sits in the injected principle list (`ops/build-runner/worker.mjs:84-85`). No playbook step routes here, so reaching it depends on the session choosing it. |
| 2 | `disable-model-invocation: true`: never model-invoked, opened only by path when applied | doesn't apply here | `docs/agents/jul-253/tailscale-proof/final/events.jsonl:118-119` | `SKILL.md:4`; `.agents/skills/poteto-mode/SKILL.md:103-113` | No invocation switch exists in the mapping. Julia injects poteto-mode as authoritative (`worker.mjs:85`), and a subagent loaded this leaf through the skill tool anyway. |
| 3 | Make the compiler reject impossible states | used differently | `ops/mastra-agents/reviewer/src/mastra/contracts.ts:225-236` | `SKILL.md:9` | Reviewer uses zod `discriminatedUnion` under `strict` (`ops/mastra-agents/reviewer/tsconfig.json:8`); the build path is plain `.mjs`, rejected by runtime throws (`ops/build-runner/jobs.mjs:67-80`). |
| 4 | Make the compiler reject unhandled variants | not used | `ops/build-runner/transport.mjs:64-66` | `SKILL.md:9` | Absent in Julia's code: no exhaustiveness check outside vendored pstack scripts (`poteto-mode/scripts/watch-pr/render.ts:17`), and an unknown protocol falls into the messages branch. |
| 5 | Prefer defining errors and special cases out of existence over proliferating handlers | used as intended | `ops/build-runner/catalogue.mjs:259-276` | `SKILL.md:9` | Models with no published price, context or dispatchable wire are refused at catalogue build, and an ambiguous route throws (`catalogue.mjs:290-297`), so no downstream handler exists. |
| 6 | For TypeScript syntax, see the `typescript-best-practices` skill | used as intended | `docs/agents/jul-253/tailscale-proof/final/events.jsonl:116-117` | `SKILL.md:9` | That run loaded typescript-best-practices first and this leaf second, in that order. Nothing in poteto-mode or the host prompt routes to the TypeScript skill. |
| 7 | Brand semantic primitives: each id gets its own type, branded intersection or newtype | not used | `ops/mastra-agents/reviewer/src/mastra/contracts.ts:3-12` | `SKILL.md:11` | Every identifier is the same `nonempty` string schema and no `__brand` exists anywhere in the repo, so a card id and a run id stay interchangeable. |
| 8 | Validate once at creation, then trust the type downstream | used differently | `ops/mastra-agents/reviewer/src/mastra/agent-activity.ts:65-85` | `SKILL.md:11` | Stored rows are untyped JSON, so every read re-parses them. The JS ledger likewise re-checks on insert (`ops/build-runner/ledger.mjs:5-19`) instead of trusting a created value. |
| 9 | If two arguments share a primitive type but mean different things, brand them | not used | `scripts/publish-pr.mjs:68-72` | `SKILL.md:11` | `assertApproved(owner, repo)` takes two bare strings and checks the joined pair; no call site keeps them apart by type. |

## Ranked findings

Ranked by how much each one changes whether a card can be proven and whether a card stops. Every citation was checked.

1. **A criterion about the live site cannot be proven from inside a run.** The session may not deploy (`pstack-session.mjs:54`), the review tree is a static `git archive` of the candidate (`worker.mjs:192-203`), and Chromium will not launch in the sandbox (JUL-269 `pstack-checklist.md:14-15`). JUL-269's single criterion required the live site. Review 1 called it `wrong thing tested`, review 2 marked it `unproven`, and the card stopped after $5.269533176. The run's own Investigation reached the same conclusion: a live-site criterion can only pass on the preview or after deploy, and rewriting that rule is Todd's call (`jobs/jul-269-aee236e60da67d7efe4cda30d58b1a67/worktree/.builder-home/investigation/root-cause.md:40`).

2. **The proof folder omits the raw evidence the review skill tells the reviewer to inspect.** `writeReviewProof` exports card, criteria, checklist, playbook, decisions, changed files, diff and history only (`worker.mjs:166-181`). Exit codes, browser transcripts, screenshots, design sketches and `root-cause.md` stay in `.builder-home`, which is excluded from the candidate diff (`pstack-session.mjs:54`). `adversarial-review/SKILL.md:45` asks for raw checked outputs and exit codes. Correct work can therefore be marked unproven, and JUL-269's remediation recorded exactly that gap (`jobs/jul-269-aee236e60da67d7efe4cda30d58b1a67/worktree/.builder-home/investigation/root-cause.md:36`).

3. **A good-faith `proven` is silently downgraded when the proof filename is short.** `parseVerdicts` rewrites `proven` to `unproven` unless the named file resolves relative to the review-tree root (`evidence-review.mjs:118-120`, `worker.mjs:151-158`). The prompt's example invites a relative name, and both JUL-269 rounds wrote `"proofFile":"checklist.md"`; only JUL-268, with `.builder-home/proof/diff.patch`, could have passed. Neither JUL-269 verdict was downgraded, because both were already `wrong thing tested` or `unproven`, but the trap is one word away from starting the stop path on a correct claim.

4. **The required playbook comes only from the card label, so a better playbook choice fails review.** `requiredBuildPlaybook` knows Feature, Refactoring, Bug fix, Perf issue and Hillclimb (`evidence-review.mjs:5-19`). The JUL-270 real run chose Bug fix for a reported defect on a Feature-labelled card, and review 1 marked every criterion unproven on that alone (`docs/agents/jul-252/proof/review-playbook-adherence-2026-10-25/README.md:15-25`). Figure-it-out, prototype and diagnosis runs have no label that makes them valid.

5. **A checklist tick is not a proof signal.** `checklist.mjs:3-8` has only finished, skipped and pending, so a step whose text says "Partial … FLAG: browser surface inconclusive" parses as finished and shows done on the dashboard (JUL-269 `pstack-checklist.md:14-15`). The skipped-step rule is prose in the reviewer prompt (`evidence-review.mjs:150`), and the runs apply it inconsistently: JUL-268 passed round 1 with Feature steps 1 and 2 skipped (`jobs/jul-268-54fc4689956a25fb7b4aa774ba0c93ae/pstack-checklist.md:3-4`), while JUL-269 failed on surface, not adherence. Proving rests on one reviewer's judgment.

6. **The decision log and checklist are outside the candidate diff, although AGENTS.md says they are committed.** The host fixes them at `.builder-home/pstack/decisions.tsv` and excludes `.builder-home/` (`pstack-session.mjs:54`, `worker.mjs:99-101`), while `AGENTS.md:11` commits the trail under `docs/agents/<card>/`. The reviewer is told to read `checklist.md` and `diff.patch` (`evidence-review.mjs:150`); `decisions.tsv` is exported but never named. The reasoning behind a card is not provable, and the two trails can disagree.

7. **Most of pstack is unreachable from a card.** Only poteto-mode, the selected playbook and adversarial-review are injected (`pstack-session.mjs:56`, `worker.mjs:26-27`, `:84-85`). 961 of 2,544 instructions are not used, and only 483 are used as intended. `disable-model-invocation: true` is unread, so any leaf can be opened by the model anyway; JUL-253's run loaded two TypeScript skills through the skill tool (`docs/agents/jul-253/tailscale-proof/final/events.jsonl:116-119`). No review axis checks any of it.

8. **Design, diagnosis and grounding work is invisible to the reviewer.** Architect sketches, `how` explanations, `why` investigations and `interrogate` findings live under `.builder-home` or the worktree. Only `decisions.tsv` can carry them into review, and the reviewer is not pointed at it. JUL-252's two design candidates and JUL-269's `root-cause.md` were real work the verdict could not see.

9. **The failure path has no return to design or a fresh build.** `nextStage` hard-codes review → remediation → review → investigation → stopped (`evidence-review.mjs:50-60`, `worker.mjs:298-318`). A wrong architecture or an unprovable criterion can only spend both reviews and stop. The only route back is Todd restarting the card.

10. **Setup can stop a card before its first turn.** `createPstackSession` throws `unknown pstack model` for any id not in the hydrated catalogue (`pstack-session.mjs:28-40`), including `inherit-parent` and `auto`, which the skill calls valid and which fill all 17 lines of `~/.agents/pstack-models.md`. The machine avoids it only because `ops/build-runner/pstack-models.md` was hand-edited to real slugs. A model-table edit stops a card with no build, no checklist and no evidence.

11. **CI's no-fakes check is stricter than the rule it encodes.** `scripts/check-no-fakes.mjs:98-116` supports changed-since-base, but the CI test calls it with `all: true` (`scripts/no-fakes-repository.test.mjs:6-8`). A card can be stopped by a pre-existing fake it did not introduce.
